Non-tunable parameters (derived, per-release, constants, identity) are listed in Appendix A.

## 2. Policy and data provenance

Policy: `/Users/boy/projects/yellowback-workspace/yb-calibration/policy/real-data-2026-10.toml` (sha256 of the parsed policy `86bc8a55939f80ae…`), used verbatim:

```toml
# Real-data policy (data agent, 2026-10-03): policy/default.toml with the data-informed keys set
# from the M7 real data in data/local/ (docs/real-data-2026-10.md §9, decisions D-RD-D5). Every
# other key is the default's, unchanged. Use: ybcal recommend --policy policy/real-data-2026-10.toml
# ybcal risk policy — the owner's risk appetite (PLAN §2.4, §5, §12).
#
# Copy this file, edit it, and pass it with `--policy FILE`. Every value is a STARTING TOLERANCE for
# the owner to confirm, not a decision. Section headers only group keys: every key below is a field
# of `ybcal.config.Policy`, and keys are unique across sections. A table whose name is itself a
# Policy field (e.g. [risk.max_bad_debt_prob]) is that field's value.
#
# Units are in the comment on each line. Probabilities are fractions (0.01 = 1 %). Shares are
# fractions of hashrate or attestor weight. Money in policy is in USD (floats are fine here: the
# policy is a tolerance, never a consensus quantity).

[general]
materiality = 0.20                 # min relative improvement of a param's primary metric to move off its current value (§2.3, §12.2)
seed = 20261003                    # default RNG seed for every run (overridden by --seed)
require_real_data_for_lock = true  # lock-readiness needs real-data provenance for every locked param (§7)

[risk]
class_heterogeneity_max = 0.50     # max relative spread of P(bad debt) within one class before a split/merge is suggested (§5.3)

sigma_mult_at = "median"           # σ multiplier assumed when sizing base ratios: "median" | "p90" (§5.3)
```

## 3. Parameters

### G1 — Price medians

Study status: **ok** · G1 [space]: space: 281 candidates; 284 evaluated (3 cached, 1 worker) in 6371.5 s
#### <a id="p-pFastWindow"></a>§3.1 `pFastWindow` — KEEP — policy unmeetable in this environment (design note G1-ENV-1)

**96 blocks (2 h) → 96 blocks (2 h)** · locked / locked · confidence high · provenance real-data
> **Policy unmeetable in this environment (attack_share_min, max_no_price_hours; design note G1-ENV-1).** the largest pool holds 72% of the quotes (52% of blocks) and sets every median alone (P ≥ 1.00 per block): attack_share_min (34%) cannot be met by any window set; no window set meets max_no_price_hours (6 h/yr): the lowest background NO_PRICE is 1205 h/yr (current 1753); the rule keeps the sets within 5% of it (≤ 1266 h/yr), then applies J. Least-harm choice on no_price_h_per_year. Exposure: background NO_PRICE 1220 h/yr with the policy's tagging set (real sequence alone 1011; 4 largest keys tag 75; every key 0); the largest pool holds 72% of the quotes and captures pFast / pMid / pSlow in 1.3 / 14.2 / 16.3 h; if it withholds quotes NO_PRICE follows in 0.8 h and lasts until 21 h after it resumes.

- **What it controls:** Fast price-median window. Read by: PRICE-1, PRICE-2, HALT-3, SIGMA-1, MINT-10.
- **Decision rule:** Among window sets whose every median needs a colluding hash share ≥ attack_share_min (34%) to control it (V16, exact binomial) and in which a 34% coalition moves pMint up or pClaim down by half its bias in ≤ 5 % of attack blocks (simulated), with background NO_PRICE ≤ max_no_price_hours (6 h/yr) and HALT-3 still firing within a day on ≥ halt_recall_floor (90%) of 70 % one-day crashes: minimise J = CVaR95%(pClaim 90 % crash lag)/current + λ·E[pMint − true | pump-dump]/current (λ = 0.5). Keep the current windows unless J improves by more than materiality (20%); ties go to the current values. Min-fills follow (L9).
- **Binding constraint:** environment-limited: the largest pool holds 72% of the quotes (52% of blocks) and sets every median alone (P ≥ 1.00 per block): attack_share_min (34%) cannot be met by any window set; no window set meets max_no_price_hours (6 h/yr): the lowest background NO_PRICE is 1205 h/yr (current 1753); the rule keeps the sets within 5% of it (≤ 1266 h/yr), then applies J — least-harm rule: current violates max_no_price_hours; nearest feasible within materiality of the best (1.242 vs 1.05) chosen over the best (minimal change)
- **Sensitivity:** the G1 objective is moderate in pFastWindow across 1–6 hours: it rises as pFastWindow increases.
- **Why not the neighbours:** -1 step (48 blocks (1 h)): violates attack_share_min, max_no_price_hours; +1 step (144 blocks (3 h)): violates attack_share_min, max_no_price_hours.
- **Locked:** Locked: changes only through a new parameter set keyed by start height (K10, L8, W19).

pFastWindow sets the fast price median (pFast): the lower median of the last pFastWindow blocks' quote tags. pMint is the minimum of the three medians, so pFast is what lets the minting price fall quickly in a crash; HALT-3 compares it with pMid; SIGMA-1 measures volatility on it; MINT-10 compares attested prices with it (rules: PRICE-1, PRICE-2, HALT-3, SIGMA-1, MINT-10). Current 96 blocks (2 h); recommended 96 blocks (2 h) — verdict CHANGE (provenance real-data, confidence high).

Decision rule: Among window sets whose every median needs a colluding hash share ≥ attack_share_min (34%) to control it (V16, exact binomial) and in which a 34% coalition moves pMint up or pClaim down by half its bias in ≤ 5 % of attack blocks (simulated), with background NO_PRICE ≤ max_no_price_hours (6 h/yr) and HALT-3 still firing within a day on ≥ halt_recall_floor (90%) of 70 % one-day crashes: minimise J = CVaR95%(pClaim 90 % crash lag)/current + λ·E[pMint − true | pump-dump]/current (λ = 0.5). Keep the current windows unless J improves by more than materiality (20%); ties go to the current values. Min-fills follow (L9).

At the current windows the crash-lag CVaR is 311.2 h, pump overpricing 169 bps, the smallest controlling coalition 35.7% of hash, background NO_PRICE 1752.6 h/yr (131.1 h per 6-hour feed outage) and HALT-3 recall on a one-day 70 % crash 100%.

Binding: environment-limited: the largest pool holds 72% of the quotes (52% of blocks) and sets every median alone (P ≥ 1.00 per block): attack_share_min (34%) cannot be met by any window set; no window set meets max_no_price_hours (6 h/yr): the lowest background NO_PRICE is 1205 h/yr (current 1753); the rule keeps the sets within 5% of it (≤ 1266 h/yr), then applies J — least-harm rule: current violates max_no_price_hours; nearest feasible within materiality of the best (1.242 vs 1.05) chosen over the best (minimal change).

the G1 objective is moderate in pFastWindow across 1–6 hours: it rises as pFastWindow increases.

Neighbours: 48 violates attack_share_min, max_no_price_hours; 144 violates attack_share_min, max_no_price_hours.

Locked: changes only through a new parameter set keyed by start height (K10, L8, W19).

#### <a id="p-volStep"></a>§3.5 `volStep` — KEEP

**48 blocks (1 h) → 48 blocks (1 h)** · locked / locked · confidence high · provenance real-data
- **What it controls:** Sampling step of the volatility estimator (pFast samples). Read by: SIGMA-1.
- **Decision rule:** volWindow/volStep minimise the coefficient of variation of σ̂ (pFast-based) in a stationary calm regime, subject to: σ̂ reaching 90 % of a calm → turbulent shift within max_sigma_lag_blocks (4,032 blocks, median path), the K12 cap trap after a 6-hour feed outage ending within the same bound, and volStep ≥ pFastWindow / 2 (samples of the pFast median no finer than half its window, D-RD-AUD-11). Keep the current pair unless the CV improves by more than materiality (20%).
- **Binding constraint:** lower-CV pairs violate sampling (16), k12_trap_le_max_sigma_lag (32), max_sigma_lag_blocks (20); materiality: best improvement 9.2% <= materiality 20%
- **Sensitivity:** the calm σ̂ CV is flat between 12 and 1 hours; the calm σ̂ CV rises moderately above 1 hours.
- **Why not the neighbours:** -1 step (36 blocks): violates sampling; +1 step (60 blocks (1.2 h)): inadmissible (vol_step_divides).
- **Locked:** Locked: changes only through a new parameter set keyed by start height (K10, L8, W19).

#### <a id="p-sigmaRefBps"></a>§3.6 `sigmaRefBps` — CHANGE

**10,000 bps (100 %) → 18,000 bps (180 %)** · locked / locked · confidence high · provenance real-data
- **What it controls:** Reference annualised volatility; multiplier = σ/σref clamped; 0 = multiplier fixed at 1. Read by: SIGMA-1, M14.
- **Decision rule:** sigmaRefBps = the median σ̂ of the realised-volatility ensemble, measured on simulated pFast (D-WP3-6) at the recommended volWindow/volStep, rounded down to a multiple of 500 bps (so the unclamped median multiplier is ≥ 1×). Keep the current value while its median ratio σ̂/sigmaRef lies in the M14 band [1×, 1.5×] and the rule value is within materiality (20%) of it.
- **Binding constraint:** M14 band / rule: current ratio 1.84× lies outside the M14 band [1, 1.5]
- **Sensitivity:** the median σ̂/sigmaRef ratio is steep in sigmaRefBps across 25–300 %: it falls as sigmaRefBps increases.
- **Why not the neighbours:** -1 step (17,500 bps (175 %)): equal on the primary metric (ties go to the current value); +1 step (18,500 bps (185 %)): equal on the primary metric (ties go to the current value).
- **Locked:** Locked: changes only through a new parameter set keyed by start height (K10, L8, W19).

#### <a id="p-valveBlocks"></a>§3.14 `valveBlocks` — KEEP (owner decision L7)

**6 blocks → 6 blocks** · excluded / patch-release · confidence low · provenance judgement
> **Policy unmeetable in this environment (; design note G5-DN-VALVE).** no value in the searched range meets the policy in the real environment. Least-harm choice on the study's least-harm metric. Exposure: not quantified.

> **Owner decision L7: the value is kept.** The evidence points to **12** (BLOCKED) because constraint valve_minority violated at the current value. Risk of keeping: at the kept value the study's constraint(s) valve_minority fail; the study's metrics, kept → evidence: valve.split_blocks 6 → 12, valve.minority_trip 0.009169 → 3.29e-05, valve.attack_trip 1 → 0.875, valve.capstuck 2.958e-06 → 0.04282.
- **What it controls:** Work valve: blocks of work over a rejected root that switch enforcement off (node-local, L7). Read by: ACT-7, BLK-2.
- **Decision rule:** Owner decision L7: the value is kept; the study still ran. valveBlocks (node-local): the shortest split (blocks to rejoin) with natural false trips ≤ max_valve_false_trips_per_year, a non-enforcing minority's trip probability ≤ valve_minority_trip_max (per race, or within valve_attack_days of a sustained race attack on the real block sequence) and, when enforcers are a genuine minority (stock share 1 − detection_drop_share), a chance ≤ 0.05 of ending stuck at the 64-note cap instead of rejoining; KEEP unless > materiality. When nothing qualifies: the smallest attack trip probability that keeps the note-cap bound (BLOCKED, least harm).
- **Binding constraint:** constraint valve_minority violated at the current value
- **Sensitivity:** valveBlocks is set by a design choice / fixed bound; no sweep.
- **Why not the neighbours:** -1 step (5 blocks): violates valve_minority; +1 step (7 blocks): violates valve_minority.
- **Change path:** Excluded: never hashed or read by a consensus rule; may change in a patch release.

#### <a id="p-claimThresholdBps"></a>§3.15 `claimThresholdBps` — CHANGE

**11,000 bps (110 %) → 12,250 bps (122.5 %)** · locked / locked · confidence high · provenance real-data
- **What it controls:** Collateral ratio below which a matured vault is claimable. Read by: RED-4, RED-5.
- **Decision rule:** claimThresholdBps = the smallest threshold (250-bps lattice) at which the claimant margin at the first RED-4(a) trigger — collateral less FEE-1 and the network fee, valued at the true price (claimant_model hold: a YEC holder keeps it; sell: sold into the bid-side depth less the policy slippage), minus the YED burned — is ≥ claimant_min_profit_bps at claim_margin_stat (p10: nine triggered claims in ten pay; worst ensemble member, D-RD-COL-6); KEEP unless more than materiality lower; BLOCKED (highest margin) when no threshold within bounds clears it.
- **Binding constraint:** constraint claim_incentive violated at the current value
- **Sensitivity:** claim.mean_bps is steep in claimThresholdBps across 107.5–150 %: it rises as claimThresholdBps increases.
- **Why not the neighbours:** -1 step (12,000 bps (120 %)): violates claim_incentive; +1 step (12,500 bps (125 %)): equal on the primary metric (ties go to the current value).
- **Locked:** Locked: changes only through a new parameter set keyed by start height (K10, L8, W19).

| claim.bad | 0.2513 | 0.0727 |
| claim.forfeit_bps | 549.6 | 1,592.2 |

**claimThresholdBps** — the collateral ratio at pClaim below which a matured vault is claimable (RED-4(a)) and the claimant's payout margin (RED-5) (rules RED-4, RED-5). Change 11000 → 12250. Decision rule: claimThresholdBps = the smallest threshold (250-bps lattice) at which the claimant margin at the first RED-4(a) trigger — collateral less FEE-1 and the network fee, valued at the true price (claimant_model hold: a YEC holder keeps it; sell: sold into the bid-side depth less the policy slippage), minus the YED burned — is ≥ claimant_min_profit_bps at claim_margin_stat (p10: nine triggered claims in ten pay; worst ensemble member, D-RD-COL-6); KEEP unless more than materiality lower; BLOCKED (highest margin) when no threshold within bounds clears it. Binding: constraint claim_incentive violated at the current value. At current → recommended: mean claimant margin (bps) 313 → 1486; show-up rate 65.6% → 90.8%; already bad at trigger 25.1% → 7.3%; owner forfeit (bps) 550 → 1592. claim.mean_bps is steep in claimThresholdBps across 107.5–150 %: it rises as claimThresholdBps increases. Design note G3-DN2: RED-5 residual is always 0 under RED-4(a) — The claimant cap uses the same threshold that made the vault underwater, so a RED-4(a) claimant takes the whole collateral; an absent owner forfeits everything above the debt (mean forfeit at the first trigger: 550 bps of the debt). Design note G3-DN3: pClaim lag eats the claim margin — pClaim = max(pMid, pSlow) lags a falling price, so when a vault first shows underwater the claimant's expected margin is 313 bps of the debt at the current threshold (show-up rate 66%; already bad debt at the trigger: 25%). Evidence: real YEC price data; confidence high. Locked: changes only through a new parameter set keyed by start height (K10, L8, W19).

Outer class ends (shortest and longest lock) are product choices: verified, not tuned — KEEP while MINT-2 contiguity and the CLTV height bound hold. Binding: verification: every constraint holds (locktime). At current: heterogeneity A 1.95; C 0.34. classMax[2] is verified, not swept for a metric. Design note G3-DN1: No liquidation before lockHeight + grace (fact 1.5-1) — The vault script admits only the owner path until claimHeight, so the base ratio must cover the whole term's drawdown. P(bad debt) at the shipped ratios: A 0.46%, B 7.04%, C 30.75% (tolerances 0.50%, 1.00%, 2.00%); the lowest reachable within the registry bounds: A 0.34%, B 6.01%, C 26.11%. At the upper bound each class meets its tolerance only for terms up to A 73 d, B 0 d, C 0 d (class maxima 90, 365, 1825 d), and a vault that is bad at the claim opening is short by 23%, 40%, 68% of its debt on average. A bad vault is not yet a realised loss: nobody may claim it below the debt, it stays claimable if the price recovers, and YED holders bear the gap only through the peg (D-RD-AUD-3). Design note G3-DN6: Term heterogeneity inside the classes — P(bad debt) rises with the term inside each class (heterogeneity A 1.95, B 1.28, C 0.34 against 0.5); with three fixed classes (NUM_CLASSES) and one ratio per class, long terms are under-collateralised relative to short ones. Evidence: real YEC price data; confidence high. Locked: changes only through a new parameter set keyed by start height (K10, L8, W19).

#### <a id="p-baseRatioBps-1"></a>§3.23 `baseRatioBps[1]` — CHANGE — policy unmeetable in this environment (design note G3-DN1)

**40,000 bps (400 %) → 62,500 bps (625 %)** · locked / locked · confidence high · provenance real-data
> **Policy unmeetable in this environment (bad_debt_B; design note G3-DN1).** the vault script admits no liquidation before lockHeight + grace, so the base ratio must cover the whole term's drawdown, and YEC's ≈ 160–180 %/yr volatility over 1 week – 1 quarter (≈ 115 % over a year) puts the needed ratio far above the registry bound for terms of months to years (docs/decisions.md D-RD-COL-4). Least-harm choice on pbad.B. #### <a id="p-abandonBlocks"></a>§3.26 `abandonBlocks` — KEEP (owner decision W21 (D-R-12))

**34,560 blocks (30 d) → 34,560 blocks (30 d)** · locked / locked · confidence high · provenance real-data
> **Owner decision W21 (D-R-12): the value is kept.** The study's evidence agrees.
- **What it controls:** ENFORCEMENT set continuously this long = module abandoned (yed_sweep); >= grace (W21). Read by: L10, L12, TPL-1, TPL-2, MP-1, W21. Classified locked by K10 wording: it gates TPL-1/2, MP-1 and yed_sweep (docs/decisions.md D-2).
- **Decision rule:** Owner decision W21 (D-R-12): the value is kept; the study still ran. abandonBlocks = the smallest value ≥ max(grace, runbook) — runbook = signalWindow (detect) + signalWindow (W19) + 16,128 (M14) + runbook_operator_buffer_blocks — with P(false abandonment per year) ≤ max_false_abandon_prob at the expected enforcing share (G5 drift mixture), rounded up to whole days; KEEP unless the current value violates the bound or the rule value is more than materiality smaller.
- **Binding constraint:** lower bound max(grace, runbook) = 34560 (grace binds)
- **Sensitivity:** P(false abandonment) is 1.2e-237/yr at the recommended value and falls geometrically with each extra signal window, so the binding constraint is the runbook / grace lower bound, not false abandonment; every extra day of abandonBlocks is one more day vaults stay unsweepable if the developers are truly gone.
- **Why not the neighbours:** -1 step (33,408 blocks (29 d)): inadmissible (abandon_ge_grace); +1 step (35,712 blocks (31 d)): equal on the primary metric (ties go to the current value).
- **Locked:** Locked: changes only through a new parameter set keyed by start height (K10, L8, W19).

**abandonBlocks** — blocks of continuous ENFORCEMENT halt after which the module counts as abandoned and owners may sweep (L10/L12, TPL-1/2, MP-1, yed_sweep; W21 ≥ grace) (rules L10, L12, TPL-1, TPL-2, MP-1, W21). Keep 34560. Decision rule: Owner decision W21 (D-R-12): the value is kept; the study still ran. abandonBlocks = the smallest value ≥ max(grace, runbook) — runbook = signalWindow (detect) + signalWindow (W19) + 16,128 (M14) + runbook_operator_buffer_blocks — with P(false abandonment per year) ≤ max_false_abandon_prob at the expected enforcing share (G5 drift mixture), rounded up to whole days; KEEP unless the current value violates the bound or the rule value is more than materiality smaller. Binding: lower bound max(grace, runbook) = 34560 (grace binds). At current: abandonBlocks (days) 30; lower bound 34560; margin (blocks) 0; P(false abandon)/yr 1.2e-237; max tolerated dev absence (days) 12.0. P(false abandonment) is 1.2e-237/yr at the recommended value and falls geometrically with each extra signal window, so the binding constraint is the runbook / grace lower bound, not false abandonment; every extra day of abandonBlocks is one more day vaults stay unsweepable if the developers are truly gone. Dev-absence variants: abandonment fires before returning developers can finish the freeze-then-fix runbook for 14 d, 30 d, 60 d, 90 d; if they never return, vaults stay unsweepable for 30 days. Design note G4-DN1: Abandonment tolerates only a short developer absence — With abandonBlocks 34560 (30 d), developers who return after more than 12 days cannot ship a freeze-then-fix set (M14 lead 16128 + buffer 4608) before owners may sweep: abandonment and a slow fix race. Evidence: real YEC price data; confidence high. Locked: changes only through a new parameter set keyed by start height (K10, L8, W19).

Decision rule: feeMin: verify — the MINT-5 floor 4·feeMin fits a minMint class-C vault at worst_price_usd (§1.4), and the owner of a minMint vault (any class, minted at the reference or the worst price) whose collateral has fallen to the claim threshold still gains by redeeming (FEE-1 ≤ 1 − 10⁴/claimThresholdBps of the collateral); else the nearest value that passes. <details><summary>Notes (4)</summary>

- Design note: an entity with 25% of the seated weight holds 34.8% of a bundle's weight when selected, so it spans qLow in 93% of bundles at the current value (proposal §7.2 states the rule for the *selected* attestors). Griefing only (aMint down → over-collateralisation); theft (aMint up) needs > 67%: P = 0.000. A seat-splitting adversary (seats of just over bondMin) moves aMint up with P ≥ ½ from 7 of 9 seats and down from 3; any qLow above one third lets 6 of 9 seats steal (D-RD-ATT-11), so griefing resistance is not bought with cheaper theft.

**1,500 bps (15 %) → 1,500 bps (15 %)** · locked / locked · confidence high · provenance real-data
- **What it controls:** Tag-vs-attestation gap above which minting refuses (to calibrate, A7). Read by: MINT-10.
- **Decision rule:** divergeBpsAttest (D-RD-ATT-3): the smallest value, rounded up to 100 bps, at which MINT-10 refuses at most max_mint10_refusal_prob of honest mints in calm — the gap between the pools' pFast and the attestors' aMint, both built by the shipped agents (median of the venues) on the real venue disagreement; KEEP when the current value is within materiality of it. **minMint** — the smallest YED amount one MINT may create (MINT-2) (rules MINT-2). Keep 10000. Decision rule: minMint ∈ [max(4·feeMin collateral at worst_price_usd, size where the feeMin floor pushes the round-trip fee share above max(max_fee_share_small, the class's proportional share) at the reference price), maxMint]; KEEP inside the range, else the nearest admissible $10 step. Binding: inside [5000, 100000]: verification (4·feeMin at the worst price / maxMint). At current: fee share A 1.45%; B 1.25%; C 0.80%; floor binds above $/YEC 0.80. fee_share.A@worst is steep in minMint across 5000–30000 cents: it falls as minMint increases. Admissible range: [5000, 100000] (lower: 4·feeMin at the worst price; upper: maxMint). FEE-1 is a fixed share of the collateral above the feeMin floor, so the round-trip fee is 1.45% / 1.25% / 0.80% of the debt for A/B/C at every size: minMint cannot bring class A under max_fee_share_small (design note G9-DN1). Design note G9-DN2: feeMin is denominated in YEC — Above $0.80/YEC the 0.5-YEC floor, not feeBps, sets a minMint vault's fee; at the policy's worst price the round-trip share of a minMint debt is 100% (class C). Evidence: real YEC price data; confidence high. Locked: changes only through a new parameter set keyed by start height (K10, L8, W19).

#### <a id="p-maxMint"></a>§3.66 `maxMint` — KEEP — policy unmeetable in this environment (design note G9-ENV-1)

**$10,000.00 → $10,000.00** · locked / locked · confidence medium · provenance judgement
> **Policy unmeetable in this environment (maxMint_ok; design note G9-ENV-1).** p10 daily YEC volume $700 (policy) × max_depth_fraction 10% ÷ the claim threshold allows a vault of $57, below the registry floor $1,000 and minMint $100. Least-harm choice on maxMint.liquidation_share. Exposure: a $10,000 vault liquidates $12,250 at the claim threshold = 17.5 days of p10 volume, 65× the ±2 % book depth ($188); a claimant unwinds it over days or not at all (bad debt on that vault, G3).

- **Sensitivity:** maxMint.volume_needed_usd is steep in maxMint across 200000–10000000 cents: it rises as maxMint increases.

#### <a id="p-walletConfirmations"></a>§3.71 `walletConfirmations` — CHANGE — policy unmeetable in this environment (design note G9-ENV-2)

**6 → 24** · excluded / patch-release · confidence medium · provenance judgement
> **Policy unmeetable in this environment (walletConfirmations_ok vs the top pool; design note G9-ENV-2).** the top real pool mines 52.0% of blocks (pool_shares): its catch-up probability is 1 at every z. Least-harm choice on reorg.p_catch_up. Exposure: at z = 24 the largest minority adversary (q = 30.0%: max of the policy's 30% and the second pool's 21.3%) catches up with P = 8.1e-04; the majority pool can reverse any Yellowback transaction (and VOID any mint by reorging R) at will, at the cost of its own block rewards.

- **Binding constraint:** lower bound 24 (Nakamoto catch-up ≤ max_reorg_prob)
- **Sensitivity:** reorg.p_catch_up is flat between 26 and 30; reorg.p_catch_up rises steeply below 26.

## 4. Joint sensitivity

Not run.

## 5. Design notes

### 1. One pool sets every price median (attack_share_min unmeetable) (G1-ENV-1)
*G1 · pFastWindow, pMidWindow, pSlowWindow, pFastMinFill, pMidMinFill, pSlowMinFill*

- **Finding:** No value of the searched parameters can meet attack_share_min in the real environment: the largest pool holds 72% of the quotes (52% of blocks) and sets every median alone (P ≥ 1.00 per block): attack_share_min (34%) cannot be met by any window set; no window set meets max_no_price_hours (6 h/yr): the lowest background NO_PRICE is 1205 h/yr (current 1220); the rule keeps the sets within 5% of it (≤ 1266 h/yr), then applies J.
- **Evidence:** exposure at the chosen value: background NO_PRICE 1220 h/yr with the policy's tagging set (real sequence alone 1011; 4 largest keys tag 75; every key 0); the largest pool holds 72% of the quotes and captures pFast / pMid / pSlow in 1.3 / 14.2 / 16.3 h; if it withholds quotes NO_PRICE follows in 0.8 h and lasts until 21 h after it resumes; no_price_h_per_year chosen / current / best: 1220 / 1220 / 1220
- **Consequence:** The least-harm value is recommended; the residual exposure above is accepted, not removed.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** arm the attestation layer early (PRICE-2 revised bounds over-minting and path-(a) claims); operationally, spread hash; no window can

### 2. Background NO_PRICE far above max_no_price_hours at every window set (G1-ENV-2)
*G1 · pFastWindow, pMidWindow, pSlowWindow, pFastMinFill, pMidMinFill, pSlowMinFill*

- **Finding:** No value of the searched parameters can meet max_no_price_hours in the real environment: the largest pool holds 72% of the quotes (52% of blocks) and sets every median alone (P ≥ 1.00 per block): attack_share_min (34%) cannot be met by any window set; no window set meets max_no_price_hours (6 h/yr): the lowest background NO_PRICE is 1205 h/yr (current 1220); the rule keeps the sets within 5% of it (≤ 1266 h/yr), then applies J.
- **Evidence:** exposure at the chosen value: background NO_PRICE 1220 h/yr with the policy's tagging set (real sequence alone 1011; 4 largest keys tag 75; every key 0); the largest pool holds 72% of the quotes and captures pFast / pMid / pSlow in 1.3 / 14.2 / 16.3 h; if it withholds quotes NO_PRICE follows in 0.8 h and lasts until 21 h after it resumes; no_price_h_per_year chosen / current / best: 1220 / 1220 / 1220
- **Consequence:** The least-harm value is recommended; the residual exposure above is accepted, not removed.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** more tagging pools (the unidentified 21 % key: 4 largest tagging → tens of h/yr), or a lower mid/slow min-fill than ⌈2W/3⌉ (L9; v3's PRICE-2 already bounds the minority attack L9 guarded against once ARMED)

### 3. One operator is pivotal for enforcement (G5-ENV-1)
*G5 · signalWindow, activationThreshold, enforcementFloor*

- **Finding:** The enforcing coalition (71.9% of blocks) contains an operator with about half the hash; without it the rest of the chain is below one half, so no coalition without it can reach the L3 majority and activation without it is impossible at any threshold >= 50 %.
- **Evidence:** coalition_share: 0.7188; coalition: ['s1NYxEmfsa4QB1oYdg7oNDf66xMuaH1dDVe', 's1iDNEgZGZLXQtpH37cVsPY3XyrouM1hoWH', 's1TbPq7Mi1dNeMpnZs9Y76LYrgAyTquBbWY']
- **Consequence:** That operator can switch enforcement off by leaving (ENFORCEMENT after about 0.8 W blocks) or fake it by signalling without enforcing (never detected by ACT-6; the valve is the only defence). No parameter changes this.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Coverage: recruit the remaining large hash (the 25 % flex block) before startHeight.

### 4. A hop can lock in a coalition that cannot hold the floors (G5-DN-HOP)
*G5 · signalWindow, activationThreshold*

- **Finding:** ACT-2 locks in on one window at the threshold, so a few days of an auto-switching pool's hash lock in a coalition whose mean share is below 60 % with probability 0.91 within the activation reach (real landscape, current set).
- **Evidence:** act.spurious_lock: 0.9062; reach_days: 60.0
- **Consequence:** Once ACTIVE that coalition flaps in and out of ENFORCEMENT (W19 windows, abandonment, defection windows).
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Procedure: publish startHeight only after the enforcing operators commit and watch yed_getactivation; or a rule change (lock in after two consecutive windows above the threshold, state.cpp:1084).

### 5. No valve length defends a 28 % stock minority against a sustained race (G5-DN-VALVE)
*G5 · valveBlocks*

- **Finding:** A matured-vault owner keeps a non-burning sweep in every stock mempool; races run back to back on the real block sequence. P(trip within the attack horizon) is 1.00 at valveBlocks 6 and 1.00 at 6; longer valves leave a genuine-minority enforcer stuck at the 64-note cap (index.h:65) instead of rejoining.
- **Evidence:** attack_trip_current: 1.0; attack_trip_least_harm: 1.0; capstuck_least_harm: 0.0
- **Consequence:** Enforcement turns off network-wide and the sweep confirms: YED loses the collateral of every vault that is swept the same way.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Coverage (with the flex 25 % enforcing, 16 blocks suffice), or a node-local patch: raise VALVE_NOTE_CAP and/or require the heavier branch's lead to persist.

### 6. No liquidation before lockHeight + grace (fact 1.5-1) (G3-DN1)
*G3 · baseRatioBps[0], baseRatioBps[1], baseRatioBps[2], classMax[2]*

- **Finding:** The vault script admits only the owner path until claimHeight, so the base ratio must cover the whole term's drawdown. P(bad debt) at the shipped ratios: A 0.46%, B 7.04%, C 30.75% (tolerances 0.50%, 1.00%, 2.00%); the lowest reachable within the registry bounds: A 0.34%, B 6.01%, C 26.11%. At the upper bound each class meets its tolerance only for terms up to A 73 d, B 0 d, C 0 d (class maxima 90, 365, 1825 d), and a vault that is bad at the claim opening is short by 23%, 40%, 68% of its debt on average. A bad vault is not yet a realised loss: nobody may claim it below the debt, it stays claimable if the price recovers, and YED holders bear the gap only through the peg (D-RD-AUD-3).
- **Evidence:** pbad_current: {'A': 0.00458329, 'B': 0.0704359, 'C': 0.307484}; pbad_at_upper_bound: {'A': 0.00343448, 'B': 0.0600592, 'C': 0.261106}; tolerance: {'A': 0.005, 'B': 0.01, 'C': 0.02}; term_reach_days_at_upper_bound: {'A': 73.125, 'B': 0, 'C': 0}; shortfall_at_upper_bound: {'A': 0.000790877, 'B': 0.0239389, 'C': 0.177182}
- **Consequence:** Classes whose tolerance cannot be met at any ratio are BLOCKED; capital efficiency of long terms collapses before the risk does.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Rule change (out of scope for tuning): a claim/top-up path during the term (e.g. RED-4 from lockHeight at a higher threshold), term-scaled ratios, or shorter maximum terms.

### 7. RED-5 residual is always 0 under RED-4(a) (G3-DN2)
*G3 · claimThresholdBps, residualMinZat*

- **Finding:** The claimant cap uses the same threshold that made the vault underwater, so a RED-4(a) claimant takes the whole collateral; an absent owner forfeits everything above the debt (mean forfeit at the first trigger: 1592 bps of the debt).
- **Evidence:** claim.forfeit_bps: 1592.18
- **Consequence:** residualMinZat only matters under RED-4(b); raising claimThresholdBps transfers more value from absent owners to claimants.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Rule change: cap the RED-4(a) claimant at debt × (1 + a bounty) and return the rest.

### 8. pClaim lag eats the claim margin (G3-DN3)
*G3 · claimThresholdBps*

- **Finding:** pClaim = max(pMid, pSlow) lags a falling price, so when a vault first shows underwater the claimant's expected margin is 1501 bps of the debt at the current threshold (show-up rate 91%; already bad debt at the trigger: 7%).
- **Evidence:** claim.mean_bps: 1501.32; claim.show_up: 0.909076; claim.bad: 0.0726795
- **Consequence:** Claimants do not show up in a steady decline; abandoned vaults drift into bad debt.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Parameter (claimThresholdBps, see its recommendation) or a rule change: a fresher claim price (e.g. pFast or the attested aClaim for RED-4(a)).

### 9. RED-4(b) never pays a claimant buying YED at par (G3-DN4)
*G3 · emergencyRatioBps*

- **Finding:** Under RED-4(b) the claimant receives the debt's worth at pClaim, the higher of the two prices, so at par it is a loss (2951 bps of the debt on average in the crash runs); it only works as a par exit for YED holders when YED trades at a discount.
- **Evidence:** emerg.b_par_loss_bps: 2951.27; emerg.benefit: -0.0258794; emerg.b_share: 0.32125
- **Consequence:** The emergency path's benefit depends on a YED discount appearing in a crash.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Rule change: pay the RED-4(b) claimant at pEmerg (or with a bounty).

### 10. The σ multiplier adds no protection at its median (G3-DN5)
*G3 · baseRatioBps[0], baseRatioBps[1], baseRatioBps[2]*

- **Finding:** The median hour-mode σ multiplier over the ensemble is 10060 bps (σ̂ on the smoothed pFast stays below sigmaRefBps, D-WP3-6), so the base ratio alone carries the drawdown budget.
- **Evidence:** sigma_median_bps: 10060
- **Consequence:** Ratios are sized at multiplier 1×; G2 (sigmaRefBps) decides whether that changes.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Parameter coupling with G2, not a rule change.

### 11. Term heterogeneity inside the classes (G3-DN6)
*G3 · classMin[0], classMin[1], classMin[2], classMax[0], classMax[1], classMax[2]*

- **Finding:** P(bad debt) rises with the term inside each class (heterogeneity A 1.95, B 1.28, C 0.34 against 0.5); with three fixed classes (NUM_CLASSES) and one ratio per class, long terms are under-collateralised relative to short ones.
- **Evidence:** heterogeneity: {'A': 1.95357, 'B': 1.2752, 'C': 0.344397}; max: 0.5
- **Consequence:** Boundary moves only shift the problem between classes.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Rule change: more classes, or a ratio that grows with the lock length.

### 12. Abandonment tolerates only a short developer absence (G4-DN1)
*G4 · abandonBlocks*

- **Finding:** With abandonBlocks 34560 (30 d), developers who return after more than 12 days cannot ship a freeze-then-fix set (M14 lead 16128 + buffer 4608) before owners may sweep: abandonment and a slow fix race.
- **Evidence:** abandonBlocks: 34560; max_absence_days: 12
- **Consequence:** A longer abandonBlocks protects slow developers but keeps vaults locked longer when they are truly gone; the parameter cannot serve both.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Policy (set dev_absence_tolerance_days) or a rule change (an explicit developer liveness signal).

### 13. Grace is drawdown exposure for short terms (G4-DN2)
*G4 · grace*

- **Finding:** The grace window adds 0.34% (A), 1.20% (B), 0.34% (C) to P(bad debt) at the shipped values because nobody can claim during it (fact 1.5-1); for class A a 30-day grace is 33–100 % of the term.
- **Evidence:** dP.A: 0.00341204; dP.B: 0.0119525; dP.C: 0.0034341; owner.p_miss: 0.00426666
- **Consequence:** Owner continuity is bought with collateral (G3 must size ratios for lock + grace).
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Rule change: allow RED-4 claims from lockHeight at a lower threshold while the owner path stays open.

14. G7 design note (early supply cap, fact 1.5-2): MINT-6 measures the cap against issuedZat = the subsidy since startHeight, so it starts at zero and grows by 1.5625 YEC a block; the cap also counts class-A supply, which bypasses it at σ = 1 (W16/W20). In the first year a typical $1,000 class-B/C mint finds no headroom on 365 days (median path; 19% of B/C attempts refused), above max_class_closed_days = 90; even supplyCapBps = 5000 leaves 362 closed days. No cap value fixes it: the fix is rule-level — an earlier issuedZat origin (e.g. the YEC supply at activation, or the subsidy since a fixed earlier height) or a cap sum that excludes cap-exempt supply. Reported for the owner; not tuned. *(G7)*
15. G7 design note (HALT-2 level, D-RD-ORA-7): P(the price falls to 1/halt within grace) at globalRatioHaltBps 25,000 is 6.0% on the bootstrap and 4.8% on the real history (daily starts) against a tolerance of 1.6%. Meeting it needs a halt near 300 %, which §1.4 forbids while class C's base ratio is 300 % (halt < every base ratio) and which W16 (recapRatioBps = 2 × halt, owner decision D-R-3) would turn into a 600 % recap gate — moving the 500 % soft-cap gate W20 pinned (D-R-11). The halt level is therefore owner-pinned in effect; the exposure is reported, not tuned. *(G7)*
16. G7 design note (cap-exempt liquidation): the worst-day liquidation demand of class-A vaults alone ($6,771, p95 of the worst crash) exceeds max_depth_fraction of the p10 daily YEC volume ($70). Class A at σ = 1 sits exactly at recapRatioBps and bypasses MINT-6 (W20), so supplyCapBps cannot bound it; only a rule change (cap-exempt supply counted against its own limit) or a higher class-A ratio would. The depth figure is a placeholder unless a depth file is loaded. *(G7)*
17. G6 design note (FEE-1 base): FEE-1 is a share of the vault's collateral, so a vault's round-trip fee as a share of its debt is 2·feeBps·baseRatio at every size — 1.63% for class A (ARMED) at the current values, above max_fee_share_small for any feeBps > 12 bps. Levying FEE-1 on the debt value (mintedCents at pMint) would make the fee class-neutral; that is a rule change, out of scope for tuning. *(G6)*
### 18. Attestor revenue floor vs fee-share cap at YEC's adoption (G6-ENV-1)
*G6 · feeBps*

- **Finding:** No value of the searched parameters can meet attestor_revenue in the real environment: at the policy adoption case AFEE-1 (25 % of FEE-1, owner-pinned D-3, over nSlots seats) reaches the attestor revenue floor only above the minMint fee-share cap.
- **Evidence:** exposure at the chosen value: attestor $33/month per seat vs the $50 floor; bondMin opportunity cost $30/month; revenue scales with adoption (mid ×5); fee.attestor_usd_month chosen / current / best: 33.41 / 33.41 / 33.41
- **Consequence:** The least-harm value is recommended; the residual exposure above is accepted, not removed.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** adoption above the low case, or the owner setting the floor to the bond's opportunity cost

19. an entity with 25% of the seated weight holds 34.8% of a bundle's weight when selected, so it spans qLow in 93% of bundles at the current value (proposal §7.2 states the rule for the *selected* attestors). Griefing only (aMint down → over-collateralisation); theft (aMint up) needs > 67%: P = 0.000. A seat-splitting adversary (seats of just over bondMin) moves aMint up with P ≥ ½ from 7 of 9 seats and down from 3; any qLow above one third lets 6 of 9 seats steal (D-RD-ATT-11), so griefing resistance is not bought with cheaper theft. *(G8: qLowBps, qHighBps)*
### 20. feeMin is denominated in YEC (G9-DN2)
*G9 · minMint*

- **Finding:** Above $0.80/YEC the 0.5-YEC floor, not feeBps, sets a minMint vault's fee; at the policy's worst price the round-trip share of a minMint debt is 100% (class C).
- **Evidence:** fee.floor_binds_above_usd: 0.8; fee_share.C@worst: 1
- **Consequence:** minMint (cents) and feeMin (zat) drift apart as the YEC price moves; a USD-stable floor needs a new parameter set after large price moves.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Rule change (feeMin in cents at pMint) or periodic re-sets.

### 21. The YEC market is too thin for any maxMint to pass the depth check (G9-ENV-1)
*G9 · maxMint*

- **Finding:** No value of the searched parameters can meet maxMint_ok in the real environment: p10 daily YEC volume $700 (policy) × max_depth_fraction 10% ÷ the claim threshold allows a vault of $57, below the registry floor $1,000 and minMint $100.
- **Evidence:** exposure at the chosen value: a $10,000 vault liquidates $12,250 at the claim threshold = 17.5 days of p10 volume, 65× the ±2 % book depth ($188); a claimant unwinds it over days or not at all (bad debt on that vault, G3); maxMint.liquidation_share chosen / current / best: 17.5 / 17.5 / 1.75
- **Consequence:** The least-harm value is recommended; the residual exposure above is accepted, not removed.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Market depth (more venues, market makers); or relax max_depth_fraction to the days a claimant may take (frontier in evidence/g9/g9_maxmint_frontier.csv). A per-vault cap cannot bound the aggregate: supplyCapBps (W20) and G3's ratios carry that.

### 22. A majority pool can reorg any confirmation depth (G9-ENV-2)
*G9 · walletConfirmations*

- **Finding:** No value of the searched parameters can meet walletConfirmations_ok vs the top pool in the real environment: the top real pool mines 52.0% of blocks (pool_shares): its catch-up probability is 1 at every z.
- **Evidence:** exposure at the chosen value: at z = 24 the largest minority adversary (q = 30.0%: max of the policy's 30% and the second pool's 21.3%) catches up with P = 8.1e-04; the majority pool can reverse any Yellowback transaction (and VOID any mint by reorging R) at will, at the cost of its own block rewards; reorg.p_catch_up chosen / current / best: 0.0008106 / 0.0008106 / 0.0001522
- **Consequence:** The least-harm value is recommended; the residual exposure above is accepted, not removed.
- **Possible fix (rule change, out of scope for tuning):** Pool diversity (the L4 launch bar: no pool above 40 %); not a wallet parameter.
